A Review on Perovskite-Type LaFeO3 Based Electrodes for CO2

Reduction in Solid Oxide Electrolysis Cells: Current Understanding
page=1 block=90767234 (x=1 y=46) width=498 height=10 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
of Structure?Functional Property Relationships
page=1 block=90767234 (x=1 y=63) width=355 height=11 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Mykhailo Pidburtnyi, Bryan Zanca, Claude Coppex, Santiago Jimenez-Villegas,

ABSTRACT: Mixed ionic and electronic conductors (MIECs) are being studied
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=196) width=338 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
extensively as a potential replacement for cermet catalysts in solid oxide cells (SOCs)?
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=203) width=338 height=10 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
collectively named for solid oxide fuel cells and solid oxide electrolysis cells?due to
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=214) width=338 height=10 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
their high activity, great stability, and low cost. Perovskite-type LaFeO3-based catalysts
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=229) width=338 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
are one of the most promising electrodes for SOCs. This review paper provides an
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=240) width=338 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
update on the chemical composition?crystal structure?physical property relationships
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=250) width=338 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and an understanding of the electrochemical reaction mechanisms in the perovskite-type
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=262) width=338 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
structure LaFeO3 electrodes. Mechanisms for electrochemical CO2 reduction on the
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=273) width=338 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
perovskite structure surface and oxygen di?usion are discussed. Furthermore, the
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=284) width=338 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
electrochemical performance enhancement strategies such as composite electrodes and nanoparticles ex-solution are brie?y
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=295) width=504 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
presented together with industrialization issues, e.g., gas impurities and stability of the interconnecting materials.
page=1 block=707161353 (x=1 y=306) width=438 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.chemmater.1c00771

1. INTRODUCTION
page=1 block=917831210 (x=1 y=341) width=86 height=6 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
High temperature solid oxide electrolysis cells (SOECs) are a
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=357) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
promising method for syngas production and organic
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=368) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
chemicals synthesis. The main advantage of the SOEC is
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=379) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
that fossil fuels burning byproducts can be used as a feeding
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=390) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
gas, which can reduce greenhouse gas emissions. The SOEC
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=401) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
can be used in the fuel cell mode to produce electricity with a
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=412) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
high e?ciency (Figure 1). Many di?erent materials are
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=423) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
currently being developed to be used as electrode catalysts
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=434) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
for SOECs. There are some requirements such as high catalytic
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=445) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
activity for redox reactions, stability in operating environments,
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=456) width=241 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and chemical stability with cell components. The most obvious
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=467) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
candidate is a widely used composite Ni?Y-doped ZrO2 (Ni-
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=477) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
YSZ) cermet electrode for solid oxide fuel cells (SOFCs).
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=489) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
However, in metal-ceramic electrodes the electrochemical
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=500) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
reactions only occur at the so-called triple-phase boundary
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=511) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
(TPB) where the ionic conducting phase (YSZ), electronic
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=522) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
conducting phase (Ni), and gas phase meet. Hence, the cell
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=533) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
performance is limited by the availablity of the TPB length.1
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=541) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Another issue is that Ni particles oxidize when water and
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=555) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
water/CO2 coelectrolysis is taking place, which leads to NiO
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=566) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
formation and a loss of electronic conductivity.2 Moreover, Ni-
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=575) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
YSZ cermets are prone to carbon formation, sulfur poisoning,
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=588) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and Ni segregation.3,4
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=597) width=85 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Mixed ionic and electronic conductors (MIECs) are being
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=10 y=610) width=231 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
developed to replace the Ni-YSZ electrode. These MIEC
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=621) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
materials demonstrate high catalytic activity, redox stability,
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=632) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
and sulfur tolerance compared to Ni-YSZ.5,6 Furthermore,
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=641) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
several MIECs have demonstrated high stability in CO2/CO at
page=1 block=1464555023 (x=1 y=654) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Read Online
page=1 block=195381554 (x=287 y=137) width=49 height=5 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=1 block=960733886 (x=265 y=568) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(SOEC) integration into renewable and nonrenewable energy
page=1 block=960733886 (x=265 y=578) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
recourses and its application.

LaFeO3 Structural Characteristics. LaFeO3 has been
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=275 y=244) width=230 height=7 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
proposed as a promising cathode for the intermediate
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=255) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
temperature (IT) SOFCs and for the electrochemical
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=266) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
reduction of CO2 in SOECs.21,22 This material displays
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=274) width=240 height=10 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
MIEC properties that can be controlled both intrinsically
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=288) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
and extrinsically.23,24 Therefore, a good understanding of the
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=296) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
crystal structure is important to understand the nature of
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=310) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
defects that control the functional properties of the LaFeO3
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=321) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
material. The structure of undoped LaFeO3 adopts an
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=332) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
orthorhombic (space group Pbnm (No. 62)) perovskite
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=343) width=241 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
arrangement at ambient temperatures and up to ?957 °C.
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=353) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
The crystal structure of LaFeO3 is shown in Figure 3.21 The
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=362) width=240 height=10 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
unit cell at room temperature has four structural units (Z = 4)
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=376) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and edge lengths of a = 5.55 Å, b = 5.56 Å, and c = 7.86 Å.21
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=384) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Tables 1 and 2 list the structural parameters of LaFeO3. There
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=398) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
are two oxygen inequivalent atomic positions; the ?rst oxygen
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=409) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(O1) is tetrahedrally coordinated by two lanthanum cations
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=420) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and two iron cations and sits within the layers of lanthanum,
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=431) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and the second oxygen (O2) is coordinated by three
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=442) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
lanthanide cations and two iron cations and rests between
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=453) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the layers of iron. The Goldschmidt tolerance factor for
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=464) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
LaFeO3 is 0.9543, which indicates that this material should
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=475) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
crystallize in the cubic perovskite structure. This deviation
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=486) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
from the cubic form is commonly caused by the A-site cation
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=497) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
being too small to ?t in the interstices of the B-site cations.18
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=505) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The structure is stabilized by neighboring octahedra tilting in
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=519) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
opposite directions; this can be described using glazer notation
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=530) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
as a+b?b? or a+a?a? depending on the space group setting
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=538) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(Pbmn vs Pnma, respectively).18 The two tilting rotations are
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=549) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
opposite in direction (out-of-phase) but equal in magnitude.
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=563) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The unit cell can be related to a pseudocubic perovskite lattice
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=574) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
by a ? b ? 21/2ap and c ? 2ap. The expansion of the unit cell is
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=583) width=240 height=10 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
related to the degree of octahedral tilting observed. The nature
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=596) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of the octahedral tilt observed in LaFeO3 has been discussed
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=607) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
by Woodward to be caused by a strong covalent bond
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=618) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
interaction between lanthanum and oxygen, and the structure
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=629) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
distorts in order to maximize the degree of orbital overlap.19
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=637) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
This displacement of La3+ cations is projected along the ab-
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=649) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
plane.
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=662) width=23 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
LaFeO3 undergoes a ?rst order structural phase transition
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=274 y=673) width=231 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
from orthorhombic to rhombohedral between ?960 and
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=683) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
?1005 °C (Figure 4).22 The nature of this nonlinear thermal
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=692) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
expansion can be explained by the octahedral tilting of the B-
page=2 block=1019298652 (x=265 y=706) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
structure is not very common due to the lack of cations with
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=629) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the ideal ionic radii on their ideal crystallographic sites. When
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=640) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the A-site cation is smaller than the Sr2+ ion, a phenomenon
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=649) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
known as octahedral tilting occurs. This leads to structural
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=662) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
modi?cations that produce interesting electrical and magnetic
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=673) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
properties. As the perovskite structure diverts from the ideal
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=684) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
cubic form, octahedral tilts act to stabilize the structure, and
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=695) width=241 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the degree or the nature of tilting has been explained using a
page=2 block=1810899357 (x=1 y=706) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
4250
page=2 block=231786897 (x=245 y=726) width=17 height=4 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=14
https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.chemmater.1c00771
page=2 block=1595282218 (x=385 y=727) width=120 height=3 fontname=NAPNKL+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=2
Chem. Mater. 2021, 33, 4249?4268
page=2 block=1595282218 (x=417 y=733) width=88 height=4 fontname=NAPNKL+AdvOT46dcae81 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
an elevated temperature.7?9 in which the entire surface is
page=2 block=1778081847 (x=1 y=18) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
electrochemically active leading to a signi?cant performance
page=2 block=1778081847 (x=1 y=32) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
improvement, unlike cermet electrodes, due to MIEC?gas
page=2 block=1778081847 (x=1 y=42) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
interface activity (dual-phase-boundary, DPB). Fluorite-type
page=2 block=1778081847 (x=1 y=54) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7

2. STRUCTURE PROPERTIES OF LaFeO3 AND ITS
page=2 block=57497692 (x=1 y=184) width=221 height=8 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
RELATED MATERIALS
page=2 block=57497692 (x=15 y=196) width=97 height=6 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The naturally occurring perovskite mineral, CaTiO3, has
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=210) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
simple origins, but the complexity built around the synthetic
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=221) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
perovskite-like structure with a general chemical formula of
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=232) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
ABX3 (A = mono-, di-, and or trivalent cations; B = tri-, tetra-,
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=243) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and pentavalent cations; X = mono-, di-, and trivalent anions).
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=254) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The ideal cubic perovskite crystal structure is built up of AX3
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=265) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
close packed layers with 1/4 of the octahedral holes ?lled with
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=276) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
B-site cations. The A-site cation is usually large (?1.4 Å) and
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=286) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
coordinated by 12 anions, and the B-site is small (?0.6 Å) and
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=297) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
octahedrally coordinated by anions. The B-site octahedra form
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=309) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a framework of corner sharing octahedra, and depending on
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=320) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the size of the B-site cation di?erent interstices spaces are
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=331) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
created that can accommodate di?erent ionic radius A-site
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=342) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
cations in the perovskite framework.
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=353) width=142 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The crystal structure of a primitive cubic perovskite ABO3 ?
page=2 block=142247393 (x=10 y=363) width=231 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
SrTiO3 is shown in Figure 2. The ideal cubic perovskite
page=2 block=142247393 (x=1 y=375) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
Figure 2. Primitive cubic perovskite (ABO3) unit cell of SrTiO3 (Sr:
page=2 block=1729779847 (x=1 y=593) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
blue, Ti: purple; O: red).
page=2 block=1729779847 (x=1 y=603) width=89 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Glazer Notation.17 The distortions in perovskites can usually
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=19) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
be explained using three common principles which include
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=32) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
size, a change in oxidation states, and Jahn?Teller e?ects.18,19
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=41) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In order to determine the stability of possible perovskite
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=274 y=54) width=231 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
materials, Goldschmidt developed a formula that uses the
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=65) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
geometric structural identity of the perovskite structure and
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=76) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the ionic radii of the A- and B-site cations (rA and rB) and
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=87) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
anion (rO).20 This Goldschmidt tolerance factor (t) is de?ned
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=96) width=240 height=10 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as
page=2 block=1431467659 (x=265 y=109) width=8 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
(rA + rO)
page=2 block=1016363973 (x=301 y=129) width=37 height=9 fontname=NAPPCO+ArnoPro-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
t =
page=2 block=1620948027 (x=277 y=136) width=13 height=7 fontname=NAPPAN+STIXGeneral-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
2 (rB + rO)
page=2 block=1442191055 (x=302 y=144) width=42 height=9 fontname=NAPPCO+ArnoPro-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(1)
page=2 block=324457684 (x=494 y=146) width=11 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
The Goldschmidt tolerance factor when equal to 1 represents
page=2 block=201576232 (x=265 y=167) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
an ideal cubic perovskite (e.g., SrTiO3). If the t number drops
page=2 block=201576232 (x=265 y=178) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
below 0.9, the structure shifts toward either an orthorhombic
page=2 block=201576232 (x=265 y=189) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
or a rhombohedral distortion caused by di?erent octahedral
page=2 block=201576232 (x=265 y=200) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tilting (e.g., CaTiO3). When the tolerance factor is greater than
page=2 block=201576232 (x=265 y=211) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
1, the perovskite structure typically shifts toward a tetragonal
page=2 block=201576232 (x=265 y=222) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
or hexagonal distortion (e.g., BaTiO3 and BaNiO3).
page=2 block=201576232 (x=265 y=233) width=202 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=3 block=1403704789 (x=1 y=155) width=317 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
While LaFeO3 is inferior compared to its doped counterparts,
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=320) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
it remains critical to understand both the parent structure and
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=331) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
how it changes when doped with di?erent cations. The alkaline
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=342) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
earth cations (Ca2+, Sr2+, and Ba2+) are the most common A-
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=351) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
site dopants. The ionic radius of calcium is very close to that of
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=364) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lanthanum, while strontium and barium are much larger. The
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=375) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
most common dopant used to manipulate the ionic
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=386) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
conductivity of this material is Sr2+,7,34,35,37,38 which has a
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=394) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
larger ionic radius (?0.1 Å larger) and forms more ionic bonds
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=407) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with oxygen. LaFeO3 has two compensation mechanisms that
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=419) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
can take place when the material is substituted with lower
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=430) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
valence cations, the iron can change its valence, oxygen
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=441) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
vacancies can be created, or some combination of both.
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=452) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
Therefore, the incorporation of these dopants is typically used
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=463) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to control the ionic conductivity of the material. Strontium has
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=474) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a larger solubility limit and increases the oxide ion conductivity
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=485) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
more than that of calcium and barium members.
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=496) width=190 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
When Sr is doped into the LaFeO3 structure
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=274 y=507) width=232 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=10
(La1?xSrxFeO3??), it appears to randomly disperse on the A-
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=518) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
site of the perovskite lattice, as no cation or oxygen vacancy
page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=529) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

page=3 block=1190820921 (x=265 y=540) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

3. OXIDE ION TRANSPORT IN LaFeO3-BASED
page=5 block=1810742349 (x=265 y=131) width=206 height=8 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
PEROVSKITES
page=5 block=1810742349 (x=279 y=142) width=61 height=6 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
A high oxygen conductivity is very important for e?cient
page=5 block=154319946 (x=265 y=157) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
SOEC operation; thus, the enhancement of the oxide ion
page=5 block=154319946 (x=265 y=168) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
conductivity and a better understanding of the transport
page=5 block=154319946 (x=265 y=179) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
mechanisms within the crystal lattice are critical. This is
page=5 block=154319946 (x=265 y=190) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
especially important for MIEC catalysts, which often have
page=5 block=154319946 (x=265 y=201) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
much lower ionic conductivity, compared to solid electrolytes.

An ex-solution method to deposit nanoparticles on the
page=10 block=198499365 (x=10 y=625) width=231 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
materials surface is to grow them directly on the surface in the
page=10 block=198499365 (x=1 y=637) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
reducing atmosphere at elevated temperatures. This technique
page=10 block=198499365 (x=1 y=648) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=10 block=198499365 (x=1 y=683) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
deviations from ideal ABO3 backbone stoichiometry. Through
page=10 block=198499365 (x=1 y=695) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
initial composition variation, particle distribution and particle
page=10 block=198499365 (x=1 y=706) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
size can be precisely controlled. It was also shown that even the

particles in nanoproximity with respect to each other remained
page=10 block=621300254 (x=265 y=591) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stable even after prolonged heat treatments. It suggests that the
page=10 block=621300254 (x=265 y=602) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
particles are ?rmly anchored on the surface, in contrast to
page=10 block=621300254 (x=265 y=614) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
particles obtained by a simple deposition. It was also shown

Degradation Mechanisms. In order to increase the ionic
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=401) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
conductivity of LaFeO3 derivatives, the number of oxygen
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=413) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
vacancies must be increased. The most common approach is to
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=424) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
partially substitute cations on the A-site. Lanthanum (3+) is
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=435) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
replaced by an ion with a similar size and lower oxidation state,
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=446) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
such as strontium (2+). It has been shown by many researchers
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=458) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
that strontium tends to segregate on the surface of the
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=469) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
perovskite that is believed to be the main reason for LSCF
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=480) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
degradation.123?126 In order to better understand the potential
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=488) width=240 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
role of Sr segregation in the LSCF catalyst degradation
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=503) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
mechanism, Oh et al.127 investigated the elemental enrichment
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=512) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
behavior of this material surface. After the sample went
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=525) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
through a heat treatment in air for 50?100 h in the
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=536) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
temperature range of 600?900 °C, submicrometer-sized
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=547) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
particles had formed on the surface of the grains. Further
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=559) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
investigation by Auger electron spectroscopy revealed that the
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=571) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
precipitate was composed of strontium and oxygen, and EDX
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=582) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
did not show any signi?cant changes in the bulk composition.
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=593) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The amount of precipitate formed increased with an increasing
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=604) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
temperature and oxygen partial pressure. A two-step
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=616) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
mechanism for the formation of Sr-rich particles was proposed.
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=627) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

Sr-enriched phase occurs. The rate of the ?rst step increases
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=650) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
with a decrease in oxygen partial pressure. The second step
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=661) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
occurs faster in oxidizing conditions. It is well-known that
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=672) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
more precipitants are formed when the oxygen partial pressure
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=683) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
increases, thus suggesting that the second step seems to be rate
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=695) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
determining.
page=11 block=854487022 (x=265 y=706) width=49 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
was achieved for a cell with in?ltrated electrodes while only

page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=30) width=65 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Another factor that can dramatically decrease the activity of
page=12 block=379478400 (x=274 y=43) width=231 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Sr-containing perovskite catalysts is Cr poisoning.129?131 The

most common source of Cr in the cells is the metallic alloys
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=65) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
that are used as cell interconnects. Cr might also be introduced
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=76) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
by some of the supporting equipment, such as heat exchangers,
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=87) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tubing, and so on. Usually poisoning is caused by the reduction

of Cr(VI) species to solid chromium particles, which can
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=109) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
deposit on the catalyst surface.132 It has been proposed that
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=118) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
this reduction is due to a kinetically controlled chemical
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=132) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
reaction between gaseous Cr(VI) species and the Sr-rich
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=143) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
segregated phases. The most common impurity phases that
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=154) width=240 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
have been observed include SrCrO4 and Cr2O3. Large (up to 6
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=165) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
?m) SrCrO4 deposits can form and block pores in the catalyst.
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=176) width=240 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
This is a problem because this phase has a low electrical
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=187) width=241 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
conductivity and low catalytic activity.
page=12 block=379478400 (x=265 y=198) width=149 height=5 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The alternative degradation mechanism has been predicted

by Ni et al.133 who observed that higher degradation was

for Ni-YSZ cermet anodes in SOFC due to Ni surface

In summary, LaFeO3-based materials have the potential to
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=265 y=420) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
replace cermet materials as electrodes in SOCs due to their
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=265 y=431) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
high catalytic activity. Here, we reviewed the following key
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=265 y=442) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
points about this material and its application in SOECs.
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=265 y=453) width=219 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
• Lanthanum ferrite-based materials crystallize in three
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=278 y=467) width=227 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
distinct crystal structures based on temperature and
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=479) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
composition, the orthorhombic phase is dominant at low
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=490) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
temperatures and in low A-site doping systems, the
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=501) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
rhombohedral phase evolves at higher temperatures or
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=512) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
with heavily doped systems, and a cubic phase is usually
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=523) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
only seen at high temperatures. The rhombohedral
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=534) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
phase seems to be the preferred crystal structure due to
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=545) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
LaFeO3 catalytic activity at ?800 °C.
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=555) width=146 height=9 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The oxygen di?usion pathways in LaFeO3 are important
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=569) width=216 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
for understanding how the structure changes at the local
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=580) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
scale when the material is operating as a catalyst at high
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=591) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
temperatures. Several possible pathways have been
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=602) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
computationally proposed using DFT studies.
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=613) width=178 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
• The catalytic performance of these materials could be
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=278 y=625) width=227 height=7 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
improved by making composites or nanoparticle ex-
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=637) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
solution techniques. Both techniques have shown
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=648) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
promise to improve the catalytic performance along
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=659) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
with lowering the cost and making more durable cells.
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=670) width=213 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Lanthanum ferrite-based materials have some disadvan-
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=683) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tages such as poisoning by impurities in the feed gas.
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=694) width=216 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Several studies have shown that properties of LaFeO3
page=14 block=1483298597 (x=289 y=705) width=216 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
In any case, strontium sulfate formation leads to a performance

decrease, which may be attributed to the active site blocking

catalytic reaction is inhibited due to the occupation of active
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=285) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
sites by carbon deposits. As coke formation is observed for
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=296) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
both perovskite structured materials without cell polarization
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=307) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and a cermet catalyst, the same behavior could be expected for
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=318) width=241 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
LaFeO3 derived materials. There are many other compounds
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=329) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
that can potentially be present in trace amounts such as AsH3,
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=340) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
H2Se, PH3, Sb, Hg, Cd, and Pb in the SOC feedstocks.162 Even
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=349) width=240 height=10 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
though the quantity of these impurities is much smaller, their
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=362) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in?uence on the SOC performance is currently unknown and
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=373) width=240 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
needs to be deeply investigated.
page=14 block=2082781203 (x=265 y=384) width=125 height=6 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tions and Sm0.8Ba0.2Cr0.95Ru0.05O3 coke
page=14 block=741730375 (x=265 y=263) width=240 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=2
La0.8Sr0.2Cr0.95Ru0.05O3
page=14 block=2077528955 (x=287 y=263) width=87 height=8 fontname=NAPOCN+AdvOT2e364b11 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=2
6. CONCLUSIONS
page=14 block=35534346 (x=265 y=405) width=81 height=6 fontname=NAPNHH+AdvOTce3d9a73 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
